## 2026-10-08: task 1, Lemma 7 at N = 3 for H in {2, 3, 4, 6}, 14 fields
- Code changes (tests pass before and after, 3/3):
  - src/lemma7_check.py: `run()` returns a dict; `--json` prints it. Records retained indices, rows tried, max weight and the Section 4 bound 96 H^(2/3) U, S1, S2, Delta (4 integer coordinates), exact N(Delta) as a decimal string, a rigorous Arb enclosure of log|N(Delta)| (python-flint 0.9.0), per-phase timing, and for every prime p <= max alpha_1 (including p = 2 and p | q, tagged "excluded"): chi(p), (2/p), class, E_p, 4E_p, v_p(N(Delta)). Text output is unchanged: rerunning the four N = 2 cases reproduces the first 22 lines of results/2026-10-08-lemma7-N2-N3.txt byte for byte, and N = 3, d = -7 reproduces 4E_p/v_p = 216/292 and 84/92.
  - The assertion H <= N was dropped. The paper assumes 1 <= H <= N (Section 4, before (4.1)); the proof of Lemma 7 uses only p > H. Runs with H > N are recorded with H_le_N = false and are outside the paper's parameter range.
  - New: src/batch_lemma7.py (parallel driver, JSONL out, refuses to overwrite), src/summarize_lemma7.py (tables), src/profile_selection.py (timing).
- Command: `python src/batch_lemma7.py results/2026-10-07-lemma7-N3-H2346.jsonl 3 2,3,4,6 5,13,17,-3,-7,-11,3,7,-1,-5,6,10,-2,-6 --procs 11` (started 2026-10-07 23:5x local, finished after midnight; file name carries the start date). Console log: results/2026-10-07-lemma7-N3-H2346.txt. Summary: `python src/summarize_lemma7.py results/2026-10-07-lemma7-N3-H2346.jsonl > results/2026-10-08-lemma7-N3-H2346-summary.txt`.
- Fields: d in {5, 13, 17, -3, -7, -11} (d = 1 mod 4), {3, 7, -1, -5} (d = 3 mod 4), {6, 10, -2, -6} (d = 2 mod 4). Both signs in each class.
- Computed (exact integer arithmetic; N = 3 only, this confirms Lemma 7 at N = 3 for these fields and H, nothing more):
  - All 56 runs: greedy selection reached M = 81 rows; Delta has integer coordinates; N(Delta) != 0.
  - Lemma 7 consequence v_p(N(Delta)) >= 4E_p held at all 127 (run, admissible prime) pairs. Minimum margin 0: equality v_p = 4E_p is common (e.g. d = -11, H = 2, p = 7: 32/32).
  - Non-admissible primes, same check: chi = +1 and (2/p) = +1: 15/15 hold. chi = +1 and (2/p) = -1: 1/76 hold (min margin -364). chi = -1 but p <= H: 4/19 hold. p | 2q: 95/99 hold.
  - Max retained weight 10, 14, 16, 20 for H = 2, 3, 4, 6 (d = -1: 10, 15, 16, 21), against the Section 4 bound 659, 864, 1047, 1372.
  - S2/S1 = 0.723, 0.421, 0.319, 0.208 for H = 2, 3, 4, 6 (all fields except d = -1).
- Inference (not a finding): the failure pattern outside the admissible class matches the proof. The proof needs p > H so the replacement rows have smaller weight, and chi(p) = -1 so Frobenius lands on sigma or sigma-tau. When either condition fails, divisibility mostly fails.
- Open question: for d = -1 (K = Q(i, sqrt 2) = Q(zeta_8)) the greedy selection retains a different set (H = 2: rows (6,2,0), (4,1,2) are dependent on earlier rows; (0,1,4), (2,2,2) are retained instead). All 13 other fields give identical retained sets at each H. Possibly the cyclotomic structure of Q(zeta_8); not investigated. Irrelevant to the paper's argument, which only needs full rank.
- Timing, this machine, single process, N = 3, d = -7, H = 2: selection 10.5 s (82 rows tried for 81 retained), determinant about 10 s. In the 11-process batch, 42 to 81 s per run.

## 2026-10-08: task 1, N = 4 profile, timing estimate, and flint port plan
- Command: `python src/profile_selection.py -7 4 2 900` (single process, idle machine). Output: results/2026-10-08-profile-selection-N4-d-7-H2.json. Calibration at N = 3: results/2026-10-08-profile-selection-N3-d-7-H2.json.
- Computed: the full greedy selection at N = 4 (M = 256), d = -7, H = 2 finished in 770 s. It tried 256 indices and retained all 256 (no rejections). Max weight 16. The largest numerator or denominator in the echelon basis reached 1318 bits. Time per retained row grows roughly like k^2 (16 rows: 1.1 s, 128: 98 s, 256: 770 s).
- Estimate (inference from N = 3, where determinant time is about equal to selection time): a pure-Python N = 4 run takes about 25 to 30 minutes per field at H = 2, and somewhat longer at larger H (more rejected rows). Going from N = 3 to N = 4 multiplied selection time by 73 (10.5 s to 770 s). The same growth would put N = 5 (M = 625) above a day per field. So the pure-Python version is fine as a reference at N <= 4, and N = 5 needs the port.
- Started the pure-Python N = 4 reference batch for the cross-check: d in {5, -7, -1, 6}, H in {2, 3}, 8 processes. Output: results/2026-10-08-lemma7-N4-fraction-reference.jsonl.
- Plan for src/lemma7_flint.py (Joe asked to see this before porting; he then asked me to continue unattended, so it is written here for review):
  1. Selection modulo a prime ideal. Take primes p < 2^31 with p = 1 mod 8 and (d/p) = 1, so p splits completely in K and a, b map to residues s, t mod p. Reduce rows through one embedding (a -> s, b -> t) and run the incremental echelon mod p with numpy. If a row is independent mod p, it is independent over K (rank can only drop under reduction), so retained rows are certified.
  2. Exact certification of each rejection. A row rejected mod p could still be independent over K. For each rejected row r, solve x B_S = r_S exactly over K (B = retained rows, S = pivot columns from step 1, written as a 4k x 4k rational system via the regular representation, flint fmpq_mat.solve), then check x B = r on all columns exactly. If the check fails, the row is retained and the run is flagged. This makes the selection identical to the exact greedy rule.
  3. Delta itself by CRT. For each split prime, compute det mod p at all four embeddings (a, b) -> (+-s, +-t), solve for the four coordinates of Delta mod p, and combine primes by CRT until the product exceeds twice a rigorous bound on |coordinates|. The bound comes from Hadamard's inequality at the complex embeddings, evaluated in Arb (row norms as in (5.1), with exact entries). Then N(Delta) = Delta sigma(Delta) tau(Delta) sigma-tau(Delta), computed exactly.
  4. Second method for N(Delta) (rule 6, also used routinely at N <= 4): flint fmpz_mat.det of the 4M x 4M integer matrix of the regular representation, whose determinant equals N_{K/Q}(Delta).
  5. Cross-checks: retained sets, S1, S2, Delta coordinates and N(Delta) must agree exactly with the Fraction version on all N = 2, 3 runs and on the N = 4 reference runs. The Fraction version stays the reference.
  6. New check enabled by having Delta: Lemma 7 states Delta in p^{E_p} R, which is stronger than p^{4E_p} | N(Delta). Check the p-adic valuation of the gcd of Delta's four coordinates against E_p directly. This can also be done now for the N = 3 Fraction runs, since their Delta coordinates are stored.

Not checked: that it compiles, `#print axioms`, or that each Lean lemma matches the paper's lemma it is named after. That needs a Lean build (task 5, optional part).

## 2026-10-08: task 4, prime-bias sums (descriptive context; tests no lemma)
- Done by a sub-agent following CLAUDE.md; I re-checked one number independently (below).
- Command: `.venv/Scripts/python src/prime_bias.py --dmax 100000 --procs 3 --date 2026-10-08` (722.6 s). Outputs: results/2026-10-08-prime-bias-table.csv, results/2026-10-08-prime-bias-summary.txt, results/2026-10-08-prime-bias-splus-vs-ell-1e4/1e5/1e6.png, results/2026-10-08-prime-bias-hist.png.
- EXPLORATORY (float64) for the bulk sums. chi_D(p) is exact integer arithmetic (validated on 557 pairs against sympy kronecker_symbol and flint jacobi). The 20 smallest S_+ at X = 10^6 were recomputed in Arb (128-bit) and agree with float64 to 9e-16.
- My independent spot check (sympy primerange + kronecker_symbol, float): S_+(D, 10^4) for D = -163, 6888, 5 is 2.6403237038, 2.3557029146, 3.2097338418, equal to the table to all 10 printed digits.
- Computed (60,786 fundamental discriminants, |D| <= 10^5; S_+ = sum over p <= X with chi_D(p) = +1 of log p / p):
  - S_+ - (1/2) log X: mean -0.853, -0.860, -0.863 and sd about 0.39-0.40 at X = 10^4, 10^5, 10^6. Range at 10^6: -2.276 to +0.131.
  - Smallest S_+ at all three X: D = 6888 = 2^3*3*7*41 (S_+ = 4.6315 at 10^6, Arb-certified). The bottom 20 are mostly positive D with many small prime factors; only -1012, -1435, -5460 are negative. D = -163 ranks 113th.
  - Smallest S_+/ell: 0.217, 0.319, 0.421 at X = 10^4, 10^5, 10^6, all at D = 79373.
- Inference (sub-agent's, which I agree with; unchecked): the -0.86 offset is mostly the Mertens-type constant (sum_{p<=X} log p/p = log X - 1.33) plus primes dividing D, which count in neither S_+ nor S_-. The low minimizers owe part of their deficit to ramified small primes, not only to inert ones. Nothing in this range comes near the regime a Siegel zero would force via (2.2); none was expected (see CLAUDE.md, Platt and Lu-Zaman-Zhao).

## 2026-10-08: task 2, interpolation lemma (Lemma 3, Corollary 4) and the kernel claim after (4.1)
- Code: src/interpolation_boxes.py (search), src/summarize_boxes.py (tables).
- Commands: `python src/interpolation_boxes.py 2 5,13,17,-3,-7,-11,3,7,-1,-5,6,10,-2,-6 8 > results/2026-10-08-interpolation-boxes-N2.jsonl`; the same with `3 ... 12` into results/2026-10-08-interpolation-boxes-N3.jsonl. Summaries: results/2026-10-08-interpolation-boxes-N2-summary.txt and -N3-summary.txt (`python src/summarize_boxes.py IN.jsonl`).
- Method: for each (t2, t3) in [0, tmax]^2, find the least t1 such that the rows with alpha_j <= t_j have rank M over K. Full rank is certified modulo a degree-one prime (rank can only drop under reduction). Minimality is certified exactly: the box with t1 - 1 has rank < M over K, computed as the exact integer rank of the regular-representation matrix (flint fmpz_mat.rank). Every minimal box in both runs carries both certificates.
- Computed:
  - Kernel claim A (ab, b, -a, -1) = 0 and "the minor in the first three columns is 4ab": both hold exactly, all 14 fields (exact arithmetic in Z[a, b]).
  - N = 2 (M = 16): minimal full-rank boxes have 16 to 20 rows (24 for d = -1). The smallest box satisfying (3.1) has 1560 rows; the Corollary 4 boxes have about 531,000 (H = 1) and 545,000 (H = 2) rows.
  - N = 3 (M = 81): minimal full-rank boxes have 81 to 100 rows. The smallest box satisfying (3.1) has 15,288 rows; the Corollary 4 boxes have about 2.7 million rows.
  - Minimal-box sets are identical across fields except d = -1 (N = 2 and N = 3) and d = 5 (N = 2 only: (8,1,0) instead of (7,1,0), which also breaks the t2 <-> t3 symmetry). Search window: t2, t3 <= 8 (N = 2), <= 12 (N = 3). Minimal boxes with larger t2 or t3 are not listed.
- Inference: for this particular A, full rank appears as soon as a box has about M monomials, so (3.1) and Corollary 4 have very large slack (factors of about 100 and 30,000 in row count at N = 3). That is expected: Lemma 3 covers every A with a rationally independent kernel, and the proof spends a factor of about 4^4 on the "4P" region and 3(N-1) per coordinate on the face interpolation. The slack does not matter to the main argument except through the constant 96 in the weight bound, which enters only through c0 and C0 in Lemma 6.
- Hand check (my reading, an inference, not a computation): I followed the proof of Lemma 3 line by line (nearest lattice point m, supporting face G, the Carathéodory vertex u with y - u in 3P, the strict distance decrease (3.5), and the Lagrange polynomial on the face) and Corollary 4's arithmetic (T_j >= 32N, T_j - 3(N-1) >= T_j/2, 4096 N^4 > (4N-3)^4). I found no gap.

## 2026-10-08: task 3, Lemma 6 at N = 5, 6, 7 (EXPLORATORY)
- Command: `python src/selection_modp.py N 1,...,N 5,-7,-1,13` for N = 5, 6, 7 > results/2026-10-08-selection-modp-N567-EXPLORATORY.jsonl. Summary: results/2026-10-08-selection-modp-N567-EXPLORATORY-summary.txt.

Retained rows are certified independent. At N = 3 the same code reproduces the exact S1, S2, max weight and rows tried (checked for d = -7, H = 2 and 6).
- Computed:
  - S1/(M H^(2/3) U): 0.426-0.455 (N = 5), 0.433-0.455 (N = 6), 0.434-0.453 (N = 7). Paper's guaranteed lower bound: c0 = 2.7e-5.
  - H * S2/S1: 1.58-1.77, 1.67-1.84, 1.69-1.89. Paper's guaranteed upper bound: C0/c0 = 7.2e6.
  - Max retained weight is under 2% of the bound 96 H^(2/3) U.
- Inference (a heuristic, not a finding): if the greedy selection meets no dependencies before weight W, the retained indices fill the simplex alpha1 + H(alpha2 + alpha3) <= W, whose volume W^3/(6H^2) must equal M. Then S1 is about M W/4 and S2 about M W/(2H). So S2/S1 is about 2/H, and S1/(M H^(2/3) U) is about 6^(1/3)/4 = 0.454. The data match the second value and approach the first (H S2/S1 rising toward 2 with N). If this holds asymptotically, the first term of (6.2), (3/4)(1 + S2/S1), drops below 1 once H > 6, against the paper's H = 86,713,344. The slack is in the constants only and does not bear on correctness.

### Step 2 (statement fidelity), reading Mathlib @ d13f23b
- DirichletCharacter.LFunction (Mathlib/NumberTheory/LSeries/DirichletContinuation.lean:61) := ZMod.LFunction χ, defined (LSeries/ZMod.lean:85) as N^(-s) Σ_j χ(j) hurwitzZeta(j/N, s): the meromorphic continuation; lemma LFunction_eq_LSeries (DirichletContinuation.lean:75) shows it equals Σ χ(n) n^(-s) for Re s > 1. So at real β in (0,1) it is the genuine L(β, χ).
- IsPrimitive (DirichletCharacter/Basic.lean:292) := conductor χ = n, with conductor = sInf of levels through which χ factors (line 246). So q in the statement is the conductor, as in the paper.
- (∀ a, (χ a).im = 0) characterizes real characters (values are 0 or roots of unity, so real means ±1 or 0). χ ≠ 1 is the nonprincipal condition. The statement of exists_absolute_real_zero_gap is the paper's Theorem 1.1 with c existential. Inference: statement is faithful; no hidden weakening found.

### Step 3: Lean-to-paper lemma map (read-only sub-agent; key points re-read by me)
- Full report saved at docs/lean-paper-map.md. Highlights (read in files):
  - Lemma 2: Structure/SumLogPrimeFactors.lean:146 `source_analytic_lemma`. Same shape as (2.1)/(2.2); C absolute, CH chosen after H and before q.
  - **Lemma 3's proof (convex geometry: nearest lattice point, supporting face, Lagrange polynomial) is not formalized.** Corollary 4's conclusion is proved instead by an algebraic-geometric multiplicity (zero) estimate on the torus G_m^4 (Differentials/TorusQuotientDimensionPosCotangentFinrankThree.lean:205 `uniform_rectangular_multiplicity`, with a projective-degree Bezout bound). Statement `actual_biquadratic_rectangle_span` (Structure/InvariantJetLinearMap.lean:471) matches Corollary 4 with T_1 = 32 H^(2/3) N^(4/3), T_2 = T_3 = 32 H^(-1/3) N^(4/3).
  - Section 4 weight bound 96 H^(2/3) U, Lemma 6 (c0 = 1/(4*97^2), C0 = 192; hypothesis N >= 18818 in place of "N large in terms of H"), (5.1), (5.2), Lemma 7, (5.6)/(5.7), (6.2), the choice of H = 86713344 and of gamma, and the final 13/16 + 1/16 step all have exact Lean counterparts (file:line in docs/lean-paper-map.md).
  - The two challenge theorems are two largely parallel proofs with the same architecture (determinant plus Bezout-type multiplicity estimate). They differ in the Bezout lemma, in the source of the Hadamard factorization (in-house vs. the patched PrimeNumberTheoremAnd files), and in bookkeeping. They are not independent at the level of ideas.
  - Added hypotheses: q != 8 (the paper's d != 2), N >= 18818, H fixed at 86713344. Statements are not weaker than the paper's.
- Inference: if the build completes and the axiom check is clean, then every step of the paper except the specific proof of Lemma 3 is machine-checked, and Lemma 3's conclusion (as Corollary 4) is machine-checked by a different proof. Our small-N computations (task 2) checked Corollary 4's conclusion directly and found huge slack.

### Step 4: explicit constant (conditional; inference, not a finding)
- docs/explicit-constant.md, src/explicit_c.py (Arb, 200-bit), src/check_classical_inputs.py (EXPLORATORY spot checks, results/2026-10-08-classical-inputs-EXPLORATORY.txt).
- Making every constant in Lemma 2 and (5.7) explicit (Davenport ch. 12; -zeta'/zeta(s) < 1/(s-1); Rosser-Schoenfeld 1962 for Mertens and Chebyshev), and optimizing N for each q (N is free subject to N >= H), the master inequality gives a contradiction when F > 0 (formula in the doc). F at fixed t = log U / log q increases with log q, so one certified evaluation covers all larger q.
- Certified (Arb): delta >= 3.0379e-4 for all q >= 3 (q != 8); >= 1.99806e-3 for q > 4e5; >= 2.58930e-3 for q > 1e10. With Platt (no real zeros for q <= 4e5, which also covers q = 8) and Lu-Zaman-Zhao Theorem 1.1 (delta > 1/5 for q <= 1e10, checked against refs/), the conditional result is (1 - beta) log q >= 0.00258 for all q >= 3.
- Spot checks (x <= 1e7; s in [1.001, 2]): all six classical inputs hold; max (s-1)(-zeta'/zeta(s)) = 0.99942 < 1.

- Step 2 change of plan: a WSL kernel update would not have helped. Landlock network rules need ABI 4 (Linux 6.7), and WSL's newer kernels are the 6.6 series (inference from kernel history, not measured). Instead: the README's systemd-run wrapper already blocks AF_UNIX via seccomp; we extended it to `RestrictAddressFamilies=~AF_UNIX AF_INET AF_INET6 AF_NETLINK AF_PACKET`. Probe (28_netblock_probe.sh, computed): with the README wrapper TCP to 1.1.1.1:443 opens and curl gets 200; with the hardened wrapper, TCP, curl, UDP and unix sockets all fail with EAFNOSUPPORT, including two levels down inside landrun.
- Run (29_comparator_nanoda.sh): our config copy differs from the repo's only in `enable_nanoda: true` (diff recorded). Output (computed): challenge and solution builds succeed (replayed from the previous in-sandbox build), "nanoda kernel accepts the solution", "Lean default kernel accepts the solution", "Your solution is okay!", exit 0, service runtime 14 min. File: results/2026-10-08-lean-comparator-nanoda-netblock.txt.
- Note: the solution's .lake was built by the 12:15 comparator run, inside the sandbox, so README assumption 2 (no build outside the checking environment) still holds.
- Log artifact: the script's own echo lines at the top of 08_comparator.log and 29_comparator_nanoda.log were overwritten by the systemd service output (two writers, separate offsets on /mnt/c). The verdict lines are intact. The overwritten details are recorded separately in the results files (22_versions.sh, 30_nanoda_record.sh).
- What remains trusted (inference): Lean's kernel and nanoda would both have to accept a wrong proof for the verdict to be wrong; plus Mathlib's definitions as used by the statement, and lean4export (shared by both kernels). IPC scoping (Landlock ABI 6) is still absent; AF_UNIX sockets are blocked, signals to the checker user's own processes are not.

  - Why the overlap is safe: Lake has no build lock (toolchain src/lean/lake/Lake/Util/Lock.lean: "Lake does not currently use a lock file"). Config i's closure is already fully built when its comparator runs, so the run only reads it; config i+1's prebuild writes only modules not yet built, which are outside config i's closure. The overlap stays inside a batch, and the prune runs only after all of a batch's runs finish.

- Joe: one specialist declined, the other did not reply. The 8.5 deposit (due 2026-10-23) goes out as the preliminary version, clearly labeled, with no changes from expert input. The replay resumes tonight, after Joe is done with the PC.

The table in the interim .md sums correctly; the copy Joe pasted had lost a column.
- Outside checks (STATED by the sources; details and locations in results/2026-10-19-external-checks-comparison.txt):
  - davegoldblatt/openai-zeta-proof-check covers one theorem, the 7/8 zeta result (QuasiRiemannHypothesis), at adc7f12, with the Lean kernel and nanoda. Both accept. Its comparator, lean4export, landrun and nanoda commits are the same as ours; the platform differs (aarch64 Docker). It ran stock nanoda with no stack setting. It does not cover SiegelZeros or any of our 7 ACCEPT_LEAN_ONLY configs. Its only overlap with our replay is QuasiRiemannHypothesis (our pilot, ACCEPT_BOTH).
  - nanoda has no stack option: the README lists only JSON config keys, none for the stack, and the checker-thread stack is the constant STACK_SIZE (16 MiB) in src/lib.rs, which ulimit -s does not change. nanoda issue #55 (open), at fd4aeeb with our exact tool commits, reports the same failures on 5 of our 7 (OptimalMaxCut, UniqueGamesTheorem, VertexCover, BinPackingGap, Laughlin). Its backtraces show a whnf/try_reduce_nat recursion that larger stacks (1 GiB, 16 GiB) do not cure. A follow-up there says nanoda master f67473b passes 14 of its 15 failing configs (fixes #49 etc.); SquareDifference still fails.
  - INFERRED: a ulimit / larger-stack retry of the 7 with nanoda 3a24072 is not expected to change any outcome (our 512 MiB test on OptimalMaxCut agrees). A retry that could change them would use a later nanoda commit. That is a method change, so it waits for Joe.
  - INFERRED: IndependentSets (batch 6) and PerfectCompleteness (batch 7) are in #55's RoundTables group, so expect nanoda stack overflows there tonight.
  - Lean v4.33.1 notes, #14807: an is_prop soundness bug gave a proof of False that "was also accepted by nanoda". So two-kernel agreement lowers kernel-bug risk but does not remove it. Our toolchain, 4.34.1, is after that fix. lean-inductive-models (nomeata) validates inductive declarations only, so it would be a partial third check. Not run.

- 2026-10-20, statement check step 5 (COMPUTED unless marked):
  - Project statement-checks/ (lakefile.toml, setup.sh, build.sh, sync.sh). Built in WSL at /home/checker/statement-checks, outside the openai/math clone.
    - Lean v4.34.1, Mathlib d13f23b and its 8 dependencies, copied with their build outputs from the clone's .lake/packages after checking each is clean and at its manifest commit. No download and no Mathlib rebuild.

  - A first smoke build was stopped by the Windows guard: free RAM 1,493 MB, because the 8 GB package copy filled WSL's page cache. After drop_caches (Windows free RAM back to 9.7 GB) it ran normally. No result was affected.
  - Lean results. Final clean build: results/2026-10-20-statement-checks/lean-build-final.log, 38 s, lake exit 0. All 32 `#print axioms` lines are exactly [propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]; the only `sorry`s are the three challenge theorems, which nothing uses. Source hashes: lean-sources.sha256.
    - QRH.lean: s = 2 satisfies the hypothesis; ζ(−2) = 0; the Dirichlet series for Re s > 1; the junk value ζ(1) = (γ − log 4π)/2 ≠ 0.
    - Dirichlet.lean: ZMod.χ₄ cast to ℂ is ≠ 1; the hypotheses hold for it and for the principal character; LFunction = LSeries for Re s > 1; the mod-1 L-function vanishes at −2.
    - Hecke.lean: trivialTop (modulus O) meets the hypotheses; LFunction χ is differentiable at every s ≠ 0, 1 for every χ, and at s = 1 whenever g₀ = 0.
    - HeckeSeries.lean, `LFunction_hasSum`, the lattice identity: for every Character and Re s > 1, LFunction χ s = Σ over (x, y) ≠ 0 of χ(x + yω) / (x² − xy + y²)^s / 6. The steps:
      - pair.f(t) = Σ_ℤ² χ(x + yω) e^(−πtQ), via the explicit bijection (a, b, e, m, n) ↔ (x, y);
      - pair.f₀ = χ(0);
      - summability, via EisensteinSeries.summable_one_div_norm_rpow;
      - Mathlib's hasSum_mellin and WeakFEPair.hasMellin.
    - HeckeHolo.lean:
      - f₀ = 0 for a proper modulus; holomorphy at s = 0 for a proper modulus;
      - LFunction trivialTop 0 = 0 (Mathlib's Γ(0) = 0). The Dedekind zeta value there is −1/6, so this is a junk value, outside the half-plane;
      - coord_surj: O = ℤ + ℤω, via integralPowerBasis;
      - orthogonality: Σ over the box of χ = 0 for residue ≠ 1, by translation averaging, so g₀ = 0. Hence every nontrivial character's LFunction is entire;
      - period_indep: changing `period` leaves LFunction unchanged on Re s > 1, and on ℂ minus {0, 1} by the identity theorem.
    - HeckeNontrivial.lean: chi7, modulus (7), residue = quadratic character mod 7 of ρ₁(z)ρ₂(z), where ρ₁ and ρ₂ send ω to 2 and to 4.
      - Built from PowerBasis.lift and minpoly ℤ ω = X² + X + 1; trivial on the 6 units (Mathlib Units.mem); ≠ 1, because its value at 2 + ω, a unit mod 7, is −1.
      - It satisfies every hypothesis, including s = 1; its L-function is entire and equals the lattice series.
  - 5c, Dedekind factorization: not attempted in Lean, after half 1 (the lattice identity) was done.
    - Reason: it needs #{(x, y) : x² − xy + y² = n} = 6 Σ_{d|n} χ₋₃(d), i.e. prime factorization in ℤ[ω] by p mod 3, which I did not find in Mathlib. My estimate is days, not hours.
    - Time box: 09:52-10:18 used, for all the Lean above. The numerical fallback ran: src/hecke_numeric.py, results/2026-10-20-statement-checks/hecke-numeric.txt.

Per-unit v2 cgroup.freeze and cgroup.kill exist under user@1000.service/app.slice. Transient unit names: run-u<N> with --wait (pipeline), run-r<hex> without.
  - New wsl-comparator/69c_lane_guard.py (root; replaces 69b for 114d). Lanes, lowest priority first: build < main-lane run < slow lane. A = MemAvailable + SwapFree.
    - A < 4500 MB: freeze the build if it runs beside another job. Thawed at A > 8000 MB or when it is alone.
    - A < 2000 MB with ≥ 2 jobs: kill only the lowest-priority job (never a query, never the only top-priority job) and write out/preempt/<config>.<lane>. With 1 job: 69b's full stop, unchanged (LIMIT line, GUARD_STOPPED; 119c policy applies).
    - Same log format plus PAUSE / RESUME / PREEMPT lines.
  - New wsl-comparator/114d_extended_replay_lanes.sh (114c's per-config steps, sandbox, timeouts, logs, classification unchanged):
    - a preempted prebuild is retried at half the threads (min 2) once no main-lane run is active (≤ 3 retries, then left for the next attempt);
    - a preempted run leaves its config without a result (rc 5; retried at the next attempt; no DONE meanwhile);
    - a 7200 s timeout's 28800 s rerun runs in the slow lane;
    - a finished 7200 s timeout log (exit 124/137 after ≥ 7000 s) is kept across restarts;
    - slow lane up to 2 configs; the lookahead build also runs while the slow lane is busy;
    - test hooks XR_W / XR_L / XR_C / XR_QD (defaults are the live paths).
